"""Cheap advisory pre-checks for robust/superrobust review."""

from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from megaplan._core import collect_git_diff_patch, intent_and_notes_block
from megaplan.types import PlanState


@dataclass
class _DiffMetadata:
    patch: str
    files: list[str]
    hunks: int
    changed_lines: int
    added_lines: dict[str, set[int]]


def _pre_check_flag(
    check: str,
    detail: str,
    *,
    severity: str = "minor",
    evidence_file: str | None = None,
    suffix: str | None = None,
) -> dict[str, str]:
    stem = check.upper().replace("-", "_")
    flag = {
        "id": f"PRECHECK-{stem}{f'-{suffix}' if suffix else ''}",
        "check": check,
        "detail": detail,
        "severity": severity,
    }
    if evidence_file:
        flag["evidence_file"] = evidence_file
```

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's my findings and recommendations:

---

## AUDIT FINDINGS & CLEANUP RECOMMENDATIONS

### 1. **HANDLERS.PY IS A MONOLITH — SPLIT BY PHASE (HIGH PRIORITY)**

`handlers.py` (2529 lines) should be reorganized into a `handlers/` subpackage with files scoped to phase groups:
- `handlers/init_plan.py`: `handle_init`, `handle_plan`, `handle_prep` (~320 lines)
- `handlers/critique_revise.py`: `handle_critique`, `handle_revise` (~300 lines)
- `handlers/gate.py`: `handle_gate`, tiebreaker logic (~180 lines)
- `handlers/finalize_execute.py`: `handle_finalize`, `handle_execute`, execution helpers (~350 lines)
- `handlers/review.py`: `handle_review` and review-related logic (~280 lines)
- `handlers/override_audit.py`: `handle_override`, `handle_audit_verifiability`, `handle_verify_human` (~220 lines)
- `handlers/shared.py`: Helper utilities (`_finish_step`, `_write_json_artifact`, etc.) (~280 lines)

This mirrors the actual workflow phases and makes the codebase navigable. Tests already exist scoped to phases; handlers should match.

---

### 2. **EXECUTION/REVIEW FAMILY NEEDS GROUPING (HIGH PRIORITY)**

Create two subpackages:

**`execute/` subpackage:**
- `execute/core.py`: `execution.py` (1077 lines) — dispatches batches, aggregates results
- `execute/quality.py`: `execution_quality.py` (191 lines) — evidence checks, git snapshots
- `execute/timeout.py`: `execution_timeout.py` (333 lines) — timeout handling & approval modes
- `execute/artifact_merge.py`: `merge.py` (part of batch result reconciliation)

**`review/` subpackage:**
- `review/core.py`: `parallel_review.py` (410 lines) — parallel review dispatch
- `review/checks.py`: `review_checks.py` (208 lines) — check summaries
- `review/mechanical.py`: `review_mechanical.py` (377 lines) — pre-checks, diff analysis
- `review/verdicts.py`: Extraction logic from `handlers.py` (`_merge_review_verdicts`, `_resolve_review_outcome`) (~140 lines)

**Rationale:** These are functionally cohesive—execution controls task execution, review controls verdict synthesis. Tests already separate them; code should too.

---

### 3. **WORKERS.PY DOES NOT OVERLAP — KEEP INTACT (NO ACTION)**

`workers.py` (1788 lines) is a **worker dispatcher/parser hub**, not a grab-bag:
- Runs CLI commands (codex, claude, hermes) and parses output
- Mocking infrastructure for all steps
- Schema validation

`hermes_worker.py` (924 lines) is a **specialist** for Hermes-only output parsing (JSON extraction, tool invocation). No real overlap—keep both.

`parallel_critique.py` and `parallel_review.py` are **orchestrators** (not workers). They call `workers.py` and `hermes_worker.py`. Clear separation—no changes needed.

---

### 4. **TIEBREAKER & CAPABILITIES — MOVE TO PROMPTS (MEDIUM PRIORITY)**

- `tiebreaker.py` (295 lines) is **purely orchestration** for tiebreaker prompts
- `prompts/tiebreaker_*.py` (5 files, ~15 lines each) are **templates**

Move `tiebreaker.py` → `prompts/tiebreaker_orchestrator.py` for co-location with templates. Same for `capabilities.py` (66 lines) — it's gate-phase logic that should be near `prompts/gate.py`.

---

### 5. **EVALUATION.PY — STAYS TOP-LEVEL, IS CORRECT (NO ACTION)**

`evaluation.py` (790 lines) is an **orchestrator for flag/robustness logic** imported by many phases. It's foundational and correctly placed. No changes needed.

---

### 6. **SCATTERED QUALITY/AUDIT MODULES — CONSOLIDATE (MEDIUM PRIORITY)**

- `audit.py` (130 lines) + `verifiability.py` (125 lines) + `iteration_pressure.py` (166 lines) + `quality.py` (404 lines) + `checks.py` (236 lines)

Create `audits/` subpackage:
- `audits/robustness.py`: `checks.py` (robustness level mapping)
- `audits/verifiability.py`: `verifiability.py` (task verification logic)
- `audits/audit_engine.py`: `audit.py` (audit reporting)
- `audits/quality_gates.py`: `quality.py` (evidence collection, deviation detection)
- `audits/iteration.py`: `iteration_pressure.py` (cycle counting logic)

These are all **post-execution/pre-finalize checks**. Grouping reduces top-level clutter.

---

### 7. **_CORE/ IS CORRECT — ADD ONE UTILITY (MINOR)**

`_core/` holds **internal infrastructure** (IO, state, workflow, registries). Correct placement. No changes needed. The underscore prefix accurately signals "internal."

---

### 8. **TESTS STRUCTURE IS ACTUALLY GOOD — ONLY SPLIT test_megaplan.py (MEDIUM PRIORITY)**

`test_megaplan.py` is **201 test functions in one file (212k lines)**. Split by phase/module:
- `test_handlers_init_plan.py`, `test_handlers_critique.py`, `test_handlers_gate.py`, `test_handlers_execute.py`, `test_handlers_review.py`
- `test_execute_core.py`, `test_review_verdicts.py`, `test_review_mechanical.py`
- `test_audits_robustness.py`, `test_audits_quality.py`

Other tests already mirror source layout (e.g., `test_parallel_review.py` → `parallel_review.py`). `test_megaplan.py` is the outlier.

---

### 9. **CLOUD/_REFERENCE/ — DELETE IT (HIGH PRIORITY)**

`cloud/_reference/` is **dead code**: old Docker configs, shell scripts, idea documents. There's a parallel `cloud/templates/healthserver.py` that's the real implementation.

**Remove:** `/megaplan/cloud/_reference/` entirely (BRIEFING_RULES.md, chain.sh, Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, ideas/, megaplan-idea*.txt, railway.toml, healthserver.py).

---

### 10. **ROOT-LEVEL MARKDOWN/PNG — MOVE OR REMOVE (LOW PRIORITY)**

- `plan.md` (32k) — development scratchpad, move to `.docs/plan-archive.md`
- `scorecard.png` (89k) — tracked in git; move to `docs/scorecard.png` or remove if superseded
- `activity-summary-2026-03-20.md` (7.7k) — dated, remove or archive to `.docs/`

These are tracked in git but clutter the root. Keep only `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `LICENSE` at root.

---

### 11. **MISC TOP-LEVEL FILES — KEEP (NO ACTION)**

- `cli.py` (49k, actually 1183 in old count) — **parser & dispatch hub**, belongs at top
- `auto.py` (24k) — **autonomous loop orchestrator**, belongs at top
- `chain.py` (22k) — **multi-plan orchestrator**, belongs at top
- `step_edit.py`, `merge.py`, `key_pool.py`, `doc_assembly.py`, `flags.py` — small, phase-independent utilities; keep at top
- `types.py`, `schemas.py` — type definitions; keep at top

---

### 12. **PROMPTS/ STRUCTURE IS SENSIBLE (NO ACTION)**

`prompts/` splits by phase (`planning.py`, `critique.py`, `review.py`, `gate.py`, `execute.py`, `finalize.py`) + tiebreaker variants. This mirrors handler phases. Correct. Only change: move orchestrators to it per #4.

---

## CONCRETE PUNCH LIST (Priority Order)

| Priority | Action | Files Affected |
|----------|--------|-----------------|
| **HIGH** | Delete `cloud/_reference/` entirely | Remove directory + contents |
| **HIGH** | Split `handlers.py` → `handlers/` subpackage (7 files) | `handlers.py` → `handlers/{init_plan, critique_revise, gate, finalize_execute, review, override_audit, shared}.py` |
| **HIGH** | Create `execute/` subpackage (4 files) | `execution.py` → `execute/core.py`; `execution_quality.py` → `execute/quality.py`; `execution_timeout.py` → `execute/timeout.py`; keep `merge.py` or move to `execute/merge.py` |
| **HIGH** | Create `review/` subpackage (4 files) | `parallel_review.py` → `review/core.py`; `review_checks.py` → `review/checks.py`; `review_mechanical.py` → `review/mechanical.py`; extract verdict logic from `handlers.py` → `review/verdicts.py` |
| **MEDIUM** | Create `audits/` subpackage (5 files) | `checks.py` → `audits/robustness.py`; `verifiability.py` → `audits/verifiability.py`; `audit.py` → `audits/audit_engine.py`; `quality.py` → `audits/quality_gates.py`; `iteration_pressure.py` → `audits/iteration.py` |
| **MEDIUM** | Move tiebreaker & capabilities → prompts | `tiebreaker.py` → `prompts/tiebreaker_orchestrator.py`; `capabilities.py` → `prompts/gate_capabilities.py` |
| **MEDIUM** | Split `test_megaplan.py` (201 tests) | Create `test_handlers_*.py`, `test_execute_*.py`, `test_review_*.py`, `test_audits_*.py` |
| **LOW** | Archive root markdown/png | Move `plan.md`, `scorecard.png`, `activity-summary-2026-03-20.md` to `.docs/` or remove |

**Total impact:** ~30 files created/moved, 0 files deleted from code (only `cloud/_reference/`). All imports update automatically via your IDE. Tests remain comprehensive.

## 1. Role & Mission
Your job is to drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

IMPORTANT: The `megaplan` CLI is NOT on PATH in this repo. Use the launcher `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan` for every CLI call. Verify it works before init by running `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`.

Always follow these priorities, in order:
1. The latest user direction relayed through notes or resume messages.
2. The live CLI state from `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. The workflow and breakpoint rules in this template.
4. Your own memory of earlier turns.

Always do these things:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only.
- Keep the outer conversation clean. Do not ask for routine confirmation.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing.
- Treat user notes as authoritative.

## 2. Startup
1. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show` to confirm launcher works. `execution.auto_approve` is already explicitly `true` in config — do NOT pass `--auto-approve`.
2. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan init --project-dir "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan" --robustness light "Split megaplan/handlers.py (2529 lines) into a handlers/ subpackage organized by workflow phase. Today handlers.py holds handle_init, handle_plan, handle_prep, handle_critique, handle_revise, handle_gate, handle_finalize, handle_execute, handle_review, handle_override, handle_audit_verifiability, handle_verify_human, plus shared helpers. Investigate the file during prep, then group handlers into phase-scoped modules with a shared.py for helpers. The new handlers/__init__.py must re-export every name currently imported from megaplan.handlers elsewhere so downstream imports keep working. Do not change handler behavior. Full test suite must pass."`
3. Capture the returned plan name.
4. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately after init.
5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`.

## 3. Phase Routing (light robustness)
- `init -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> done`
- No prep, no gate, no review at light robustness.
- After `revise`, the CLI state is `gated`, so next command is `finalize`.
- In auto-approve mode, after `finalize` run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`.
- After `execute` the run is done.

## 4. After Every Phase
Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`. Re-read `state`, `next_step`, `valid_next`. Read the full `notes` array if `notes_count > 0`.

## 5. Breakpoints
Emit a breakpoint only for:
- `PHASE_ESCALATE` — a non-execute phase fails after one fresh retry.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE` — 3 consecutive execute attempts without forward progress.

Format:
`BREAKPOINT: <type>`
`Plan: <name>`
`State: <state>`
`Summary: <short reason>`
`Context: <artifacts, warnings, or decision needed>`

## 6. Safeguards
- If a non-execute phase fails, retry once with `--fresh`. If it fails again, `BREAKPOINT: PHASE_ESCALATE`.
- Count execute forward progress. 3 consecutive no-progress attempts → `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_ESCALATE`.

## 10. Completion
When done, return:
`COMPLETE: megaplan run finished`
`Plan: <name>`
`Final state: <state>`
`Summary: <outcome>`
`Artifacts: <key files or reports>`
`Follow-up: <only if something remains>`

You're doing a Python refactor in /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan — a well-tested package. Goal: group scattered top-level modules into two new subpackages so the package root is less cluttered.

## What to do

Create two new subpackages and move the following files, keeping behavior identical and updating every import site:

**`megaplan/execute/` subpackage** — move these files into it (rename as shown):
- `megaplan/execution.py` → `megaplan/execute/core.py`
- `megaplan/execution_quality.py` → `megaplan/execute/quality.py`
- `megaplan/execution_timeout.py` → `megaplan/execute/timeout.py`
- `megaplan/merge.py` → `megaplan/execute/merge.py`

**`megaplan/audits/` subpackage** — move these files into it (rename as shown):
- `megaplan/audit.py` → `megaplan/audits/audit_engine.py`
- `megaplan/verifiability.py` → `megaplan/audits/verifiability.py`
- `megaplan/iteration_pressure.py` → `megaplan/audits/iteration.py`
- `megaplan/quality.py` → `megaplan/audits/quality_gates.py`
- `megaplan/checks.py` → `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`
- `megaplan/capabilities.py` → `megaplan/audits/capabilities.py`

Each subpackage needs a `__init__.py` that re-exports every name currently imported from the old module paths, so existing `from megaplan.execution import X` style imports continue working from the new paths. But DO update every import site in the codebase (under `megaplan/` and `tests/`) to use the new canonical paths (e.g. `from megaplan.execute.core import X`). The re-exports in `__init__.py` are a safety net for anything missed, not a permanent compatibility shim.

Do NOT touch `megaplan/tiebreaker.py` — a parallel refactor will move it after you finish.

## Context

- Python launcher: `./.venv/bin/python` — do NOT use bare `python` (resolves to 3.8 and breaks on `NotRequired` in typing). Every test invocation: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest ...`
- The tests directory was just reorganized: tests are now spread across files like `tests/test_init_plan.py`, `tests/test_execute.py`, `tests/test_review.py`, `tests/test_audits.py`, etc. (no more `tests/test_megaplan.py`).
- There's a `tests/conftest.py` with shared fixtures.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` was just split into a `megaplan/handlers/` subpackage with files per phase (`init.py`, `plan.py`, `execute.py`, `review.py`, etc. — note one of them happens to share the name `execute.py` with a file in your new `execute/` subpackage; that's fine since they're in different packages).
- There are 2 known pre-existing test failures in `tests/test_imported_decision_merge.py` unrelated to this refactor — don't try to fix them, just note them.

## Success criteria

1. `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -x --ignore=tests/test_imported_decision_merge.py` passes (same as before the refactor — the 2 known failures are allowed).
2. `grep -rE "from megaplan\.(execution|execution_quality|execution_timeout|merge|audit|verifiability|iteration_pressure|quality|checks|capabilities) " megaplan/ tests/` returns ONLY hits from the new subpackages' `__init__.py` re-exports — no stale import sites.
3. The old files at `megaplan/execution.py`, `megaplan/execution_quality.py`, `megaplan/execution_timeout.py`, `megaplan/merge.py`, `megaplan/audit.py`, `megaplan/verifiability.py`, `megaplan/iteration_pressure.py`, `megaplan/quality.py`, `megaplan/checks.py`, `megaplan/capabilities.py` no longer exist.
4. `megaplan/__init__.py`'s public `__all__` surface is preserved — any symbol currently re-exported from there still works.

## Approach

1. Start by running the baseline test suite to know what "green" looks like right now.
2. `grep -r "from megaplan\.execution\|from megaplan\.execution_quality\|from megaplan\.execution_timeout\|from megaplan\.merge\|from megaplan\.audit\|from megaplan\.verifiability\|from megaplan\.iteration_pressure\|from megaplan\.quality\|from megaplan\.checks\|from megaplan\.capabilities" megaplan/ tests/` to enumerate every import site you'll need to update.
3. Create the directories + `__init__.py` files.
4. `git mv` the source files to their new paths and rename. Use `git mv` so history is preserved.
5. Update imports across `megaplan/` and `tests/`.
6. Run the suite. Fix any failures caused by the move.
7. Report back with a summary of files moved, import sites updated, and test result.

Do the work end-to-end. Do not stop for confirmation at intermediate steps.

{

  "content": "\"\"\"Execute subpackage — execution, merge, timeout, and quality helpers.\n\nThis ``__init__`` re-exports every name historically imported from the\npre-refactor top-level modules (``megaplan.execution``,\n``megaplan.execution_quality``, ``megaplan.execution_timeout``,\n``megaplan.merge``) so that any lingering ``from megaplan.execute import X``\nstyle callers keep working. 1	"""Parallel Hermes review runner.
2	
3	The preloaded-template-ID convention is the preferred way to get structured
4	output from focused review agents: write the exact slot shape first, then have
5	each agent fill that file instead of inventing IDs in free-form JSON.
6	
7	This module intentionally mirrors `megaplan.parallel_critique` so the two phase
8	runners remain easy to compare and later extract into a shared utility.
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import sys
14	import time
15	import uuid
16	from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
17	from pathlib import Path
18	from typing import Any
19	
20	from megaplan._core import get_effective, read_json, schemas_root
21	from megaplan.hermes_worker import _toolsets_for_phase, clean_parsed_payload, parse_agent_output
22	from megaplan.prompts.review import (
23	    _write_criteria_verdict_review_template,
24	    _write_single_check_review_template,
25	    parallel_criteria_review_prompt,
26	    single_check_review_prompt,
27	)
28	from megaplan.types import CliError, PlanState
29	from megaplan.workers import STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES, WorkerResult
30	
31	from megaplan.key_pool import (
32	    _load_hermes_env,
33	    _get_api_credential,
34	    resolve_model as _resolve_model,
35	    acquire_key,
36	    report_429,
37	)
38	
39	
40	def _merge_unique(groups: list[list[str]]) -> list[str]:
41	    merged: list[str] = []
42	    seen: set[str] = set()
43	    for group in groups:
44	        for item in group:
45	            if item not in seen:
46	                seen.add(item)
47	                merged.append(item)
48	    return merged
49	
50	
51	def _clean_review_check_payload(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
52	    checks = payload.get("checks")
53	    if not isinstance(checks, list):
54	        return
55	    for check in checks:
56	        if not isinstance(check, dict):
57	            continue
58	        check.pop("guidance", None)
59	        check.pop("prior_findings", None)
60	
61	
62	def _failure_reason(exc: Exception) -> str:
63	    if isinstance(exc, CliError):
64	        return exc.message
65	    return str(exc) or exc.__class__.__name__
66	
67	
68	def _run_check(
69	    index: int,
70	    check: Any,
71	    *,
72	    state: PlanState,
73	    plan_dir: Path,
74	    root: Path,
75	    model: str | None,
76	    schema: dict[str, Any],
77	    project_dir: Path,
78	    pre_check_flags: list[dict[str, Any]],
79	) -> tuple[int, dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str], float, int, int, int]:
80	    from hermes_state import SessionDB
81	    from run_agent import AIAgent
82	
83	    check_id = check["id"] if isinstance(check, dict) else getattr(check, "id")
84	    output_path = _write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, f"review_check_{check_id}.json")
85	    prompt = single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path, pre_check_flags)
86	    resolved_model, agent_kwargs = _resolve_model(model)
87	
88	    _model_lower = (resolved_model or "").lower()
89	    _reasoning_families = ("qwen/qwen3", "deepseek/deepseek-r1")
90	    _reasoning_off = (
91	        {"enabled": False}
92	        if any(_model_lower.startswith(prefix) for prefix in _reasoning_families)
93	        else None
94	    )
95	
96	    def _make_agent(m: str, kw: dict) -> "AIAgent":
97	        agent = AIAgent(
98	            model=m,
99	            quiet_mode=True,
100	            skip_context_files=True,
101	            skip_memory=True,
102	            enabled_toolsets=_toolsets_for_phase("review"),
103	            session_id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
104	            session_db=SessionDB(),
105	            max_tokens=8192,
106	            reasoning_config=_reasoning_off,
107	            **kw,
108	        )
109	        agent._print_fn = lambda *args, **kwargs: print(*args, **kwargs, file=sys.stderr)
110	        return agent
111	
112	    def _run_attempt(current_agent, current_output_path: Path) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str], float, int, int, int]:
113	        current_result = current_agent.run_conversation(user_message=prompt)
114	        payload, raw_output = parse_agent_output(
115	            current_agent,
116	            current_result,
117	            output_path=current_output_path,
118	            schema=schema,
119	            step="review",
120	            project_dir=project_dir,
121	            plan_dir=plan_dir,
122	        )
123	        clean_parsed_payload(payload, schema, "review")
124	        _clean_review_check_payload(payload)
125	        payload_checks = payload.get("checks")
126	        if not isinstance(payload_checks, list) or len(payload_checks) != 1 or not isinstance(payload_checks[0], dict):
127	            raise CliError(
128	                "worker_parse_error",
129	                f"Parallel review output for check '{check_id}' did not contain exactly one check",
130	                extra={"raw_output": raw_output},
131	            )
132	        verified = payload.get("verified_flag_ids", [])
133	        disputed = payload.get("disputed_flag_ids", [])
134	        return (
135	            current_result,
136	            payload_checks[0],
137	            verified if isinstance(verified, list) else [],
138	            disputed if isinstance(disputed, list) else [],
139	            float(current_result.get("estimated_cost_usd", 0.0) or 0.0),
140	            int(current_result.get("prompt_tokens", 0) or 0),
141	            int(current_result.get("completion_tokens", 0) or 0),
142	            int(current_result.get("total_tokens", 0) or 0),
143	        )
144	
145	    agent = _make_agent(resolved_model, agent_kwargs)
146	    try:
147	        _result, check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = _run_attempt(agent, output_path)
148	    except Exception as exc:
149	        exc_str = str(exc)
150	        if "429" in exc_str:
151	            if model and model.startswith("minimax:"):
152	                report_429("minimax", agent_kwargs.get("api_key", ""), cooldown_secs=60)
153	            elif model and model.startswith("zhipu:"):
154	                cooldown = 3600 if "Limit Exhausted" in exc_str else 120
155	                report_429("zhipu", agent_kwargs.get("api_key", ""), cooldown_secs=cooldown)
156	        if model and model.startswith("minimax:"):
157	            or_key = acquire_key("openrouter")
158	            if or_key:
159	                from megaplan.key_pool import minimax_openrouter_model
160	
161	                fallback_model = minimax_openrouter_model(model[len("minimax:"):])
162	                fallback_kwargs = {"base_url": "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1", "api_key": or_key}
163	                if isinstance(exc, CliError):
164	                    print(
165	                        f"[parallel-review] MiniMax returned bad content ({_failure_reason(exc)}), falling back to OpenRouter",
166	                        file=sys.stderr,
167	                    )
168	                else:
169	                    print(f"[parallel-review] Primary MiniMax failed ({exc}), falling back to OpenRouter", file=sys.stderr)
170	                output_path = _write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, f"review_check_{check_id}.json")
171	                agent = _make_agent(fallback_model, fallback_kwargs)
172	                try:
173	                    _result, check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = _run_attempt(agent, output_path)
174	                except Exception as fallback_exc:
175	                    raise CliError(
176	                        "worker_error",
177	                        (
178	                            f"Parallel review failed for check '{check_id}' "
179	                            f"(both MiniMax and OpenRouter): primary={_failure_reason(exc)}; "
180	                            f"fallback={_failure_reason(fallback_exc)}"
181	                        ),
182	                        extra={"check_id": check_id},
183	                    ) from fallback_exc
184	            else:
185	                raise
186	        else:
187	            raise
188	    return (
189	        index,
190	        check_payload,
191	        verified_ids,
192	        disputed_ids,
193	        cost_usd,
194	        pt,
195	        ct,
196	        tt,
197	    )

308	
309	def run_parallel_review(
310	    state: PlanState,
311	    plan_dir: Path,
312	    *,
313	    root: Path,
314	    model: str | None,
315	    checks: tuple[Any, ...],
316	    pre_check_flags: list[dict[str, Any]],
317	    max_concurrent: int | None = None,
318	) -> WorkerResult:
319	    started = time.monotonic()
320	    schema = read_json(schemas_root(root) / STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES["review"])
321	    project_dir = Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])
322	    results: list[tuple[dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str]] | None] = [None] * len(checks)
323	    criteria_payload: dict[str, Any] | None = None
324	    total_cost = 0.0
325	    total_prompt_tokens = 0
326	    total_completion_tokens = 0
327	    total_tokens = 0
328	
329	    real_stdout = sys.stdout
330	    sys.stdout = sys.stderr
331	    try:
332	        total_futures = len(checks) + 1
333	        concurrency = min(
334	            max_concurrent or get_effective("orchestration", "max_critique_concurrency"),
335	            total_futures,
336	        )
337	        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=concurrency) as executor:
338	            future_map = {
339	                executor.submit(
340	                    _run_check,
341	                    index,
342	                    check,
343	                    state=state,
344	                    plan_dir=plan_dir,
345	                    root=root,
346	                    model=model,
347	                    schema=schema,
348	                    project_dir=project_dir,
349	                    pre_check_flags=pre_check_flags,
350	                ): ("check", index)
351	                for index, check in enumerate(checks)
352	            }
353	            future_map[
354	                executor.submit(
355	                    _run_criteria_verdict,
356	                    state=state,
357	                    plan_dir=plan_dir,
358	                    root=root,
359	                    model=model,
360	                    schema=schema,
361	                    project_dir=project_dir,
362	                )
363	            ] = ("criteria", None)
364	            for future in as_completed(future_map):
365	                kind, _index = future_map[future]
366	                result = future.result()
367	                if kind == "criteria":
368	                    criteria_payload, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = result
369	                else:
370	                    index, check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = result
371	                    results[index] = (check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids)
372	                total_cost += cost_usd
373	                total_prompt_tokens += pt
374	                total_completion_tokens += ct
375	                total_tokens += tt
376	    finally:
377	        sys.stdout = real_stdout
378	
379	    ordered_checks: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
380	    verified_groups: list[list[str]] = []
381	    disputed_groups: list[list[str]] = []
382	    for item in results:
383	        if item is None:
384	            raise CliError("worker_error", "Parallel review did not return all check results")
385	        check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids = item
386	        ordered_checks.append(check_payload)
387	        verified_groups.append(verified_ids)
388	        disputed_groups.append(disputed_ids)
389	
390	    if criteria_payload is None:
391	        raise CliError("worker_error", "Parallel review did not return a criteria verdict payload")
392	
393	    disputed_flag_ids = _merge_unique(disputed_groups)
394	    disputed_set = set(disputed_flag_ids)
395	    verified_flag_ids = [flag_id for flag_id in _merge_unique(verified_groups) if flag_id not in disputed_set]
396	    return WorkerResult(
397	        payload={
398	            "checks": ordered_checks,
399	            "verified_flag_ids": verified_flag_ids,
400	            "disputed_flag_ids": disputed_flag_ids,
401	            "criteria_payload": criteria_payload,
402	        },
403	        raw_output="parallel",
404	        duration_ms=int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000),
405	        cost_usd=total_cost,
406	        session_id=None,
407	        prompt_tokens=total_prompt_tokens,
408	        completion_tokens=total_completion_tokens,
409	        total_tokens=total_tokens,
410	    )
411

1	"""Review check registry and helpers.
2	
3	This largely mirrors :mod:`megaplan.audits.robustness`; the shared helper body is an
4	extraction candidate once critique and review settle on the same abstraction.
5	"""
6	
7	from __future__ import annotations
8	
9	from dataclasses import dataclass
10	from typing import Any, Final
11	
12	
13	@dataclass(frozen=True)
14	class ReviewCheckSpec:
15	    id: str
16	    question: str
17	    guidance: str
18	    category: str
19	    default_severity: str
20	    tier: str
21	
22	
23	REVIEW_CHECKS: Final[tuple[ReviewCheckSpec, ...]] = (
24	    ReviewCheckSpec(
25	        id="coverage",
26	        question="Does the diff cover every concrete failing example, symptom, or 'X should Y' statement in the original issue?",
27	        guidance=(
28	            "List each concrete failing example, symptom, or behavioral requirement in the issue. "
29	            "For each one, cite the diff line that addresses it and flag anything still uncovered. "
30	            "Also watch for: "
31	            "(a) Opt-in vs default: if the issue wants new default behavior but the diff adds a flag, flag it. "
32	            "(b) Accepted-change violations: if the issue accepts a behavior change, flag code that preserves the old behavior anyway. "
33	            "(c) Secondary sentences: treat extra expectations in side phrases as real requirements. "
34	            "Do NOT flag a verification test referenced by the plan that doesn't exist in the repo as a coverage gap. "
35	            "Test files are a task-level no-modify structural constraint already accepted by the gate; if it still matters, downgrade to status: significant. "
36	            "Focus blocking coverage findings on source changes that miss concrete issue symptoms."
37	        ),
38	        category="completeness",
39	        default_severity="likely-significant",
40	        tier="core",
41	    ),
42	    ReviewCheckSpec(
43	        id="placement",
44	        question="Is the fix placed where the bad state originates, or only where the symptom surfaces?",
45	        guidance=(
46	            "Do a backward trace, not just a forward one. Start from the changed code and ask: where is the bad value first introduced? "
47	            "If your fix runs AFTER the bad state already exists, you are papering over a downstream symptom — flag it, even if the symptom from the issue goes away. "
48	            "Then identify at least two distinct code paths by which a user could trigger this bug (e.g., direct function call, alternate entry point, reflection/registry lookup, import-time evaluation). "
49	            "Does your fix block ALL of them, or only the one the issue demonstrates? If only one, flag it. "
50	            "If the issue involves parsing, serialization, or transformation, verify the fix is at the source of the bad representation, not a downstream consumer that compensates for it. "
51	            "Ask: would a maintainer reviewing this PR expect the fix to live here, or somewhere more fundamental?"
52	        ),
53	        category="correctness",
54	        default_severity="likely-significant",
55	        tier="core",
56	    ),
57	    ReviewCheckSpec(
58	        id="adjacent_calls",
59	        question="Do adjacent callers, sibling usage sites, or downstream consumers still exhibit the bug or mishandle the new values?",
60	        guidance=(
61	            "First: look at other code that calls the changed function, uses the same surrounding pattern, or is registered alongside the changed class. "
62	            "For each sibling site, check whether the reported bug would still affect it and flag any adjacent cases left unfixed. "
63	            "Specifically: if the patch adds a new class/handler alongside existing siblings, enumerate every method those siblings override and ask whether ours should override them too. "
64	            "Second: for any value the patch returns or constructs in a new/error path, trace where it flows next. "
65	            "Check whether downstream consumers actually handle that value correctly, and whether any new sentinel or return-value semantics introduce inconsistencies. "
66	            "Flag anything a maintainer would mark as incomplete."
67	        ),
68	        category="completeness",
69	        default_severity="likely-significant",
70	        tier="core",
71	    ),
72	    ReviewCheckSpec(
73	        id="simplicity",
74	        question="Is this the smallest change that would make the issue's reported example work, and does it match how sibling code solves similar problems?",
75	        guidance=(
76	            "First: describe the smallest possible change that would resolve the reported symptom, then compare that baseline against the actual diff. "
77	            "Every extra line in the patch should be justified against a specific requirement from the issue — if it cannot, flag it as unjustified scope. "
78	            "Second: find 2-3 structurally similar classes or functions in the same module. "
79	            "Enumerate the methods, overrides, or conventions they share, then compare the new code against that pattern. "
80	            "If the patch diverges from the neighborhood's conventions, the divergence must be explicitly justified; otherwise flag it. "
81	            "Over-engineered patches (large refactors when a 1-line fix would do, new config options the issue did not request, backward-compat shims against changes the issue explicitly accepted) should be flagged."
82	        ),
83	        category="maintainability",
84	        default_severity="likely-significant",
85	        tier="core",
86	    ),
87	)
88	
89	_CHECK_BY_ID: Final[dict[str, ReviewCheckSpec]] = {check.id: check for check in REVIEW_CHECKS}
90	_CORE_REVIEW_CHECKS: Final[tuple[ReviewCheckSpec, ...]] = tuple(
91	    check for check in REVIEW_CHECKS if check.tier == "core"
92	)
93	# All current review checks are core. The tier field is retained for parity
94	# with `megaplan/checks.py` and future extensibility — if we add narrower
95	# specialized checks later, demote them to "extended" and they will be
96	# excluded from standard/light robustness here.
97	
98	
99	def get_check_ids() -> list[str]:
100	    return [check.id for check in REVIEW_CHECKS]
101	
102	
103	def get_check_by_id(check_id: str) -> ReviewCheckSpec | None:
104	    return _CHECK_BY_ID.get(check_id)
105	
106	
107	def build_check_category_map() -> dict[str, str]:
108	    return {check.id: check.category for check in REVIEW_CHECKS}
109	
110	
111	def checks_for_robustness(robustness: str) -> tuple[ReviewCheckSpec, ...]:
112	    if robustness in {"robust", "superrobust"}:
113	        return REVIEW_CHECKS
114	    if robustness in {"light", "tiny"}:
115	        return ()
116	    return _CORE_REVIEW_CHECKS
117	
118	
119	def build_empty_template(checks: tuple[ReviewCheckSpec, ...] | None = None) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
120	    active_checks = REVIEW_CHECKS if checks is None else checks
121	    return [
122	        {
123	            "id": check.id,
124	            "question": check.question,
125	            "findings": [],
126	        }
127	        for check in active_checks
128	    ]
129	
130	
131	_MIN_FINDING_DETAIL_LENGTH = 40
132	
133	
134	def _valid_findings(findings: Any) -> bool:
135	    if not isinstance(findings, list) or not findings:
136	        return False
137	    for finding in findings:
138	        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
139	            return False
140	        detail = finding.get("detail")
141	        flagged = finding.get("flagged")
142	        if not isinstance(detail, str) or not detail.strip():
143	            return False
144	        if len(detail.strip()) < _MIN_FINDING_DETAIL_LENGTH:
145	            return False
146	        if not isinstance(flagged, bool):
147	            return False
148	    return True
149	
150	
151	def validate_review_checks(
152	    payload: Any,
153	    *,
154	    expected_ids: tuple[str, ...] | list[str] | None = None,
155	) -> list[str]:
156	    raw_checks = payload.get("checks") if isinstance(payload, dict) else payload
157	    expected = get_check_ids() if expected_ids is None else list(expected_ids)
158	    if not isinstance(raw_checks, list):
159	        return expected
160	
161	    expected_set = set(expected)
162	    valid_ids: set[str] = set()
163	    invalid_expected_ids: set[str] = set()
164	    invalid_unknown_ids: set[str] = set()
165	    seen_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
166	
167	    for raw_check in raw_checks:
168	        if not isinstance(raw_check, dict):
169	            continue
170	        check_id = raw_check.get("id")
171	        if not isinstance(check_id, str) or not check_id:
172	            continue
173	
174	        seen_counts[check_id] = seen_counts.get(check_id, 0) + 1
175	        if check_id not in expected_set:
176	            invalid_unknown_ids.add(check_id)
177	            continue
178	        if seen_counts[check_id] > 1:
179	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
180	            continue
181	
182	        question = raw_check.get("question")
183	        findings = raw_check.get("findings")
184	        if not isinstance(question, str) or not question.strip():
185	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
186	            continue
187	        if not _valid_findings(findings):
188	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
189	            continue
190	        valid_ids.add(check_id)
191	
192	    return [
193	        check_id
194	        for check_id in expected
195	        if check_id not in valid_ids or check_id in invalid_expected_ids
196	    ] + sorted(invalid_unknown_ids)
197	
198	
199	__all__ = [
200	    "REVIEW_CHECKS",
201	    "ReviewCheckSpec",
202	    "build_check_category_map",
203	    "build_empty_template",
204	    "checks_for_robustness",
205	    "get_check_by_id",
206	    "get_check_ids",
207	    "validate_review_checks",
208	]
209

1	"""Cheap advisory pre-checks for robust/superrobust review."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import ast
6	import re
7	from dataclasses import dataclass
8	from pathlib import Path
9	from typing import Any
10	
11	from megaplan._core import collect_git_diff_patch, intent_and_notes_block
12	from megaplan.types import PlanState
13	
14	
15	@dataclass
16	class _DiffMetadata:
17	    patch: str
18	    files: list[str]
19	    hunks: int
20	    changed_lines: int
21	    added_lines: dict[str, set[int]]
22	
23	
24	def _pre_check_flag(
25	    check: str,
26	    detail: str,
27	    *,
28	    severity: str = "minor",
29	    evidence_file: str | None = None,
30	    suffix: str | None = None,
31	) -> dict[str, str]:
32	    stem = check.upper().replace("-", "_")
33	    flag = {
34	        "id": f"PRECHECK-{stem}{f'-{suffix}' if suffix else ''}",
35	        "check": check,
36	        "detail": detail,
37	        "severity": severity,
38	    }
39	    if evidence_file:
40	        flag["evidence_file"] = evidence_file
41	    return flag
42	
43	
44	# Paths that should never be counted as part of a source patch. These are
45	# workspace/infrastructure files that `git add -N . && git diff HEAD` will
46	# happily include but that the mechanical pre-checks should ignore.
47	_DIFF_NOISE_PREFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = (
48	    ".megaplan/",       # megaplan's own plan/critique/execution metadata
49	    "megaplan/.megaplan/",
50	    ".git/",            # git internals (shouldn't appear, but defensive)
51	    "__pycache__/",     # compiled python
52	    ".pytest_cache/",
53	    ".mypy_cache/",
54	    ".ruff_cache/",
55	    ".tox/",
56	    "node_modules/",
57	    ".venv/",
58	    "venv/",
59	    ".hermes/",         # hermes agent state
60	)
61	_DIFF_NOISE_SUBSTRINGS: tuple[str, ...] = (
62	    "/__pycache__/",
63	    "/.megaplan/",
64	    "/node_modules/",
65	)
66	
67	
68	def _is_diff_noise(path: str) -> bool:
69	    """Return True if the diff entry is workspace/infrastructure noise, not source."""
70	    normalized = path.replace("\\", "/")
71	    if any(normalized.startswith(prefix) for prefix in _DIFF_NOISE_PREFIXES):
72	        return True
73	    if any(substr in normalized for substr in _DIFF_NOISE_SUBSTRINGS):
74	        return True
75	    if normalized.endswith((".pyc", ".pyo")):
76	        return True
77	    return False
78	
79	
80	def _parse_diff_metadata(patch: str) -> _DiffMetadata:
81	    if patch in {
82	        "No git changes detected.",
83	        "Project directory is not a git repository.",
84	        "git not found on PATH.",
85	        "git diff timed out.",
86	    }:
87	        return _DiffMetadata(
88	            patch=patch,
89	            files=[],
90	            hunks=0,
91	            changed_lines=0,
92	            added_lines={},
93	        )
94	
95	    files: list[str] = []
96	    file_set: set[str] = set()
97	    added_lines: dict[str, set[int]] = {}
98	    changed_lines = 0
99	    hunk_count = 0
100	    current_file: str | None = None
101	    current_new_line = 0
102	    skip_current_file = False
103	
104	    for raw_line in patch.splitlines():
105	        if raw_line.startswith("diff --git "):
106	            match = re.match(r"diff --git a/(.+) b/(.+)", raw_line)
107	            current_file = match.group(2) if match else None
108	            skip_current_file = bool(current_file and _is_diff_noise(current_file))
109	            if current_file and not skip_current_file and current_file not in file_set:
110	                file_set.add(current_file)
111	                files.append(current_file)
112	                added_lines.setdefault(current_file, set())
113	            continue
114	
115	        if skip_current_file:
116	            continue
117	
118	        if raw_line.startswith("@@ "):
119	            match = re.match(r"@@ -\d+(?:,\d+)? \+(\d+)(?:,\d+)? @@", raw_line)
120	            if match:
121	                current_new_line = int(match.group(1))
122	                hunk_count += 1
123	            continue
124	
125	        if current_file is None:
126	            continue
127	
128	        if raw_line.startswith("+++ ") or raw_line.startswith("--- "):
129	            continue
130	        if raw_line.startswith("+"):
131	            changed_lines += 1
132	            added_lines.setdefault(current_file, set()).add(current_new_line)
133	            current_new_line += 1
134	            continue
135	        if raw_line.startswith("-"):
136	            changed_lines += 1
137	            continue
138	        if raw_line.startswith(" "):
139	            current_new_line += 1
140	
141	    return _DiffMetadata(
142	        patch=patch,
143	        files=files,
144	        hunks=hunk_count,
145	        changed_lines=changed_lines,
146	        added_lines=added_lines,
147	    )
148	
149	
150	def _is_test_path(path: str) -> bool:
151	    pure = Path(path)
152	    name = pure.name.lower()
153	    parts = {part.lower() for part in pure.parts}
154	    return (
155	        "tests" in parts
156	        or name.startswith("test_")
157	        or name.endswith("_test.py")
158	        or ".test." in name
159	        or ".spec." in name
160	    )
161	
162	
163	def _source_touch_flags(metadata: _DiffMetadata) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
164	    if not metadata.files:
165	        return [
166	            _pre_check_flag(
167	                "source_touch",
168	                "No changed files were detected in the git diff, so review cannot confirm that package source changed.",
169	                severity="significant",
170	            )
171	        ]
172	    non_test_files = [path for path in metadata.files if not _is_test_path(path)]
173	    if non_test_files:
174	        return []
175	    return [
176	        _pre_check_flag(
177	            "source_touch",
178	            "The diff only touches test-like files. Heavy review expects at least one non-test package file to change.",
179	            severity="significant",
180	            evidence_file=metadata.files[0],
181	        )
182	    ]
183	
184	
185	def _rough_issue_expectation(state: PlanState) -> int:
186	    intent_block = intent_and_notes_block(state)
187	    fenced_snippets = intent_block.count("```") // 2
188	    return max(1, fenced_snippets) * 10
189	
190	
191	def _diff_size_sanity_flags(metadata: _DiffMetadata, state: PlanState) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
192	    expected_lines = _rough_issue_expectation(state)
193	    actual_lines = metadata.changed_lines
194	    if expected_lines <= 0:
195	        return []
196	    ratio = actual_lines / expected_lines if expected_lines else 0.0
197	    if actual_lines == 0 or ratio > 3.0 or ratio < 0.3:
198	        direction = "larger" if ratio > 3.0 else "smaller"
199	        if actual_lines == 0:
200	            detail = (
201	                f"Diff size sanity check found no changed lines, versus a rough expectation of about {expected_lines} lines "
202	                f"from the issue context (files={len(metadata.files)}, hunks={metadata.hunks})."
203	            )
204	        else:
205	            detail = (
206	                f"Diff size looks {direction} than expected: changed_lines={actual_lines}, expected≈{expected_lines}, "
207	                f"ratio={ratio:.2f}, files={len(metadata.files)}, hunks={metadata.hunks}."
208	            )
209	        severity = "significant" if ratio > 3.0 or actual_lines == 0 else "minor"
210	        evidence_file = metadata.files[0] if metadata.files else None
211	        return [_pre_check_flag("diff_size_sanity", detail, severity=severity, evidence_file=evidence_file)]
212	    return []
213	
214	
215	def _iter_python_files(project_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:
216	    return [
217	        path
218	        for path in project_dir.rglob("*.py")
219	        if ".git" not in path.parts and "__pycache__" not in path.parts
220	    ]
221	
222	
223	def _parent_map(tree: ast.AST) -> dict[ast.AST, ast.AST]:
224	    parents: dict[ast.AST, ast.AST] = {}
225	    for parent in ast.walk(tree):
226	        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(parent):
227	            parents[child] = parent
228	    return parents
229	
230	
231	def _enclosing_function(node: ast.AST, parents: dict[ast.AST, ast.AST]) -> ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef | None:
232	    current = node
233	    while current in parents:
234	        current = parents[current]
235	        if isinstance(current, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
236	            return current
237	    return None
238	
239	
240	def _falsey_literal(node: ast.AST | None) -> bool:
241	    if node is None:
242	        return False
243	    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
244	        return node.value in {None, False, 0, 0.0, ""}
245	    if isinstance(node, (ast.List, ast.Tuple, ast.Set, ast.Dict)):
246	        return len(node.elts if hasattr(node, "elts") else node.keys) == 0
247	    return False
248	
249	
250	def _literal_truthiness(node: ast.AST | None) -> bool | None:
251	    if node is None:
252	        return None
253	    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
254	        return bool(node.value)
255	    if isinstance(node, (ast.List, ast.Tuple, ast.Set, ast.Dict)):
256	        return bool(node.elts if hasattr(node, "elts") else node.keys)
257	    return None
258	
259	
260	def _function_param_defaults(func: ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef) -> dict[str, ast.AST | None]:
261	    positional = list(func.args.posonlyargs) + list(func.args.args)
262	    defaults: dict[str, ast.AST | None] = {arg.arg: None for arg in positional}
263	    if func.args.defaults:
264	        for arg, default in zip(positional[-len(func.args.defaults):], func.args.defaults):
265	            defaults[arg.arg] = default
266	    for arg, default in zip(func.args.kwonlyargs, func.args.kw_defaults):
267	        defaults[arg.arg] = default
268	    return defaults
269	
270	
271	def _argument_for_param(call: ast.Call, func: ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef, param_name: str) -> ast.AST | None:
272	    positional = [arg.arg for arg in list(func.args.posonlyargs) + list(func.args.args)]
273	    if param_name in positional:
274	        index = positional.index(param_name)
275	        if index < len(call.args):
276	            return call.args[index]
277	    for keyword in call.keywords:
278	        if keyword.arg == param_name:
279	            return keyword.value
280	    return None
281	
282	
283	def _candidate_call_truthiness(project_dir: Path, function_name: str, param_name: str, func: ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef) -> list[bool | None]:
284	    truthiness: list[bool | None] = []
285	    for path in _iter_python_files(project_dir):
286	        try:
287	            tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
288	        except (OSError, SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError):
289	            continue
290	        for node in ast.walk(tree):
291	            if not isinstance(node, ast.Call):
292	                continue
293	            callee = node.func
294	            if isinstance(callee, ast.Name) and callee.id == function_name:
295	                argument = _argument_for_param(node, func, param_name)
296	                truthiness.append(_literal_truthiness(argument))
297	            elif isinstance(callee, ast.Attribute) and callee.attr == function_name:
298	                argument = _argument_for_param(node, func, param_name)
299	                truthiness.append(_literal_truthiness(argument))
300	    return truthiness
301	
302	
303	def _dead_guard_static_flags(project_dir: Path, metadata: _DiffMetadata) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
304	    flags: list[dict[str, str]] = []
305	    python_files = [path for path in metadata.files if path.endswith(".py") and metadata.added_lines.get(path)]
306	    for rel_path in python_files:
307	        path = project_dir / rel_path
308	        try:
309	            source = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
310	            tree = ast.parse(source)
311	        except SyntaxError as exc:
312	            flags.append(
313	                _pre_check_flag(
314	                    "dead_guard_static",
315	                    f"Skipped dead-guard static analysis because {rel_path} could not be parsed as Python: {exc.msg}.",
316	                    severity="minor",
317	                    evidence_file=rel_path,
318	                    suffix="PARSE",
319	                )
320	            )
321	            continue
322	        except OSError:
323	            continue
324	
325	        parents = _parent_map(tree)
326	        for node in ast.walk(tree):
327	            if not isinstance(node, ast.If):
328	                continue
329	            if node.lineno not in metadata.added_lines.get(rel_path, set()):
330	                continue
331	            if not isinstance(node.test, ast.Name):
332	                continue
333	
334	            func = _enclosing_function(node, parents)
335	            if func is None:
336	                continue
337	
338	            guard_name = node.test.id
339	            defaults = _function_param_defaults(func)
340	            if guard_name not in defaults:
341	                continue
342	            if _falsey_literal(defaults.get(guard_name)):
343	                continue
344	
345	            truthiness = _candidate_call_truthiness(project_dir, func.name, guard_name, func)
346	            if not truthiness:
347	                continue
348	            if any(value is None or value is False for value in truthiness):
349	                continue
350	
351	            detail = (
352	                f"New guard `if {guard_name}:` in {rel_path}:{node.lineno} appears unreachable by the observed call sites for "
353	                f"`{func.name}()`: every statically readable caller passes a truthy literal for `{guard_name}`."
354	            )
355	            flags.append(
356	                _pre_check_flag(
357	                    "dead_guard_static",
358	                    detail,
359	                    severity="significant",
360	                    evidence_file=rel_path,
361	                    suffix=str(node.lineno),
362	                )
363	            )
364	    return flags
365	
366	
367	def run_pre_checks(plan_dir: Path, state: PlanState, project_dir: Path) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
368	    del plan_dir
369	    metadata = _parse_diff_metadata(collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir))
370	    flags: list[dict[str, str]] = []
371	    flags.extend(_source_touch_flags(metadata))
372	    flags.extend(_diff_size_sanity_flags(metadata, state))
373	    flags.extend(_dead_guard_static_flags(project_dir, metadata))
374	    return flags
375	
376	
377	__all__ = ["run_pre_checks"]
378
```

`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py` accesses `megaplan.review_checks` as an attribute on the top-level package. Since `megaplan/__init__.py` doesn't import `review_checks` directly, this must work because some other module imports `megaplan.review_checks` which registers it as an attribute on `megaplan`. Let me check.

Actually, `megaplan/handlers/review.py` does `import megaplan.review_checks as review_checks`. After this runs, `megaplan.review_checks` is registered as an attribute of the `megaplan` package. After the refactor, the canonical path is `megaplan.review.checks`. Tests access `megaplan.review_checks` — that attribute will disappear.

I need to check: does the test currently work only because something else imports it? Yes. So after the refactor, I need to update `test_handle_review_robustness.py` to use `megaplan.review.checks` directly (or use a different accessor).

Let me also check the meta monkeypatches:

`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py:164` does `monkeypatch.setattr(megaplan.handlers, "run_parallel_review", _fail_parallel)`. Since `megaplan/handlers/__init__.py` imports `run_parallel_review`, this is patching the symbol on the handlers package. 56	def _scaffold(tmp_path: Path, *, iteration: int = 1) -> tuple[Path, Path, PlanState]:
57	    plan_dir = tmp_path / "plan"
58	    project_dir = tmp_path / "project"
59	    plan_dir.mkdir()
60	    project_dir.mkdir()
61	    (project_dir / ".git").mkdir()
62	    state = _state(project_dir, iteration=iteration)
63	
64	    atomic_write_text(plan_dir / f"plan_v{iteration}.md", "# Plan\nDo it.\n")
65	    atomic_write_json(
66	        plan_dir / f"plan_v{iteration}.meta.json",
67	        {
68	            "version": iteration,
69	            "timestamp": "2026-04-07T00:00:00Z",
70	            "hash": "sha256:test",
71	            "success_criteria": [{"criterion": "criterion", "priority": "must"}],
72	            "questions": [],
73	            "assumptions": [],
74	        },
75	    )
76	    atomic_write_json(plan_dir / "finalize.json", _build_mock_payload("finalize", state, plan_dir))
77	    atomic_write_json(plan_dir / "gate.json", {"settled_decisions": []})
78	    atomic_write_json(plan_dir / "faults.json", {"flags": []})
79	    return plan_dir, project_dir, state

98	def test_run_parallel_review_merges_check_results_in_original_order(
99	    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
100	    tmp_path: Path,
101	) -> None:

104	    criteria_payload = _build_mock_payload("review", state, plan_dir, review_verdict="approved")
105	    call_counts = {"criteria": 0, "checks": 0}
106	
107	    def fake_run_check(index: int, check: object, **kwargs: object):
108	        assert kwargs["pre_check_flags"] == [{"id": "PRECHECK-1"}]
109	        call_counts["checks"] += 1
110	        if index == 0:
111	            time.sleep(0.05)
112	        return (
113	            index,
114	            _check_payload(check, f"Checked {check.id} in detail for ordered merge coverage.", flagged=False),
115	            [f"VERIFIED-{index + 1}"],
116	            [],
117	            0.25,
118	            0,
119	            0,
120	            0,
121	        )
122	
123	    def fake_run_criteria_verdict(**kwargs: object):
124	        del kwargs
125	        call_counts["criteria"] += 1
126	        return criteria_payload, 0.5, 0, 0, 0

128	    monkeypatch.setattr("megaplan.parallel_review._run_check", fake_run_check)

130

143	    assert result.payload["criteria_payload"] == criteria_payload
